**THE TEXT TOKENS ARE CONTEXTUAL, AND THAT IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART.** A dark ground with light panels has **two** text palettes, and a class like `.tiny` is used in both — inside a card, and bare on the ground a few lines later. Hard-coding it to either leaves the other unreadable: the meetings screen shipped a caption at **1.94:1** exactly that way. So the `:root` values of `--text`/`--muted`/`--faint` are the on-the-ground ones, every light surface (`.top,.card,.box,.hdrmenu,.step,.btn,.icon,.notice,.lnk,.shot`) restates the same three names from `--panel-*`, and custom properties inherit — so `.tiny{color:var(--faint)}` is simply correct in both places and a new element cannot get it wrong. SIT asserts each surface is in that list; dropping one renders light text on a light ground. **`--line` vs `--line-strong` splits the same way**: `--line` is the 35% edge where a panel meets the dark ground, `--line-strong` is any rule INSIDE a panel, where 35% alpha simply vanishes — which is how the divider between DIGIT2AI and the product name disappeared.

**THE AUDIT WALKS THE REAL ANCESTOR CHAIN, AND AN EARLIER ONE DID NOT.** Contrast is measured against what is actually painted behind a node, compositing every translucent ancestor down to the ground. `test-header.js` had compared the login tagline against `body` — correct when the page was one colour, and after the re-theme it reported **1.42:1** for type that renders at 7.88:1, a failure the product did not have. Two other self-inflicted traps worth not re-hitting: `/serif/i` **false-passes** on the CRM's stack, which begins `ui-sans-serif`, so the font assertion had to become the positive one; and a selector capture that swallows the preceding CSS comment makes the first selector read `/* … */.top` and never match, so comments are stripped before matching — the same mistake as the login palette grep, made twice.

**THE LOOK WAS CLAUDE'S, AND ONE STYLESHEET SERVES BOTH SCREENS** (superseded above; the structure still holds) (`public/theme.css`): warm off-white `#faf9f5`, near-black text, clay `#d97757` for actions, a serif for headings and a system sans for UI. **The code and diff pane stays DARK** (`--ink-*`, `.ink`) — an editor panel inside a light app — because diff and status colours are unreadable on cream; those tokens are deliberately not theme-swapped. A second palette inside a page is how two screens drift apart, so the suite fails if either page carries one.

**ONE LANGUAGE ON THE WHOLE SCREEN — NO LINE IS STORED AS FINISHED TEXT.** Switching language relabelled the header and left the pane alone, so an English console listed "Probando / Subiendo la rama / Desplegado": every line kept the language it happened to be written in, and the step bar never redrew at all. A line is now a KEY plus arguments (`MSG` for what the console authors, `SRV` for what the server and the GitHub runner author, travelling in `detail.i18n`), the pane keeps the raw events in `shown`, and the toggle repaints all of it, redraws the step bar from `lastJob`, and re-renders a plan on screen. **Anything with no key is shown exactly as it arrived** — a model's prose, a file path, an API error — because inventing a translation for it would be worse than leaving it. The server and runner sites that must keep sending a key are asserted in the OFFLINE suite, because the browser test supplies the key itself and cannot catch it being dropped at the source (proven: dropping it passed the browser test and failed the offline one). `node verticals/speakup/test-header.js` -> **190/190** now also drives the toggle and fails if a single Spanish word survives in an English pane.

**NAMED AutoDev; "SpeakUp" IS THE WORKING NAME** (owner request 2026-09-17, on the back of the `autodev.digit2ai.com` domain). Every user-facing surface says **AutoDev** beside the DIGIT2AI lockup — the two page titles, the login title, the `.product` span on all three screens, and the manifest `name`/`short_name`, so the home-screen icon reads AutoDev. **Internal identifiers deliberately did NOT change**, exactly as BuyersLine kept `incentiva`: the mount stays `/speakup/`, the folder `verticals/speakup/`, the table prefix `su_`, every `SPEAKUP_*` env var, the cookie, the worker's `CACHE` string and the `speakup` project key in the registry. Renaming those would move the PWA scope, the session cookie and the table names for a copy change. The manifest `id` stays `/speakup/` too — it is the installed app's identity, and changing it would install a *second* app beside the first. The login tagline moved with the name: it said "Voz a texto + IA", which describes the tool this used to be, not the speak-plan-approve loop it is. The owner asked only for the login, but renaming one screen recreates the drift the theme fix had just closed — a login saying AutoDev over a header saying SpeakUp — so the name moved everywhere at once.

**THE LOGIN WAS THE LAST SCREEN ON THE OLD THEME, AND IT IS THE FIRST ONE ANYONE SEES.** `login.html` carried its own palette inline (a purple `#7b6bff` on a navy `#0a0e18`) and never linked `theme.css`, so the sign-in looked like a different product from the app behind it. It now loads the same stylesheet; the page-local block that remains exists because `theme.css` has no form rules, and it reads the SHARED TOKENS and declares **no colour of its own** — a second palette inside a page is how two screens drift apart, so the offline suite greps for one (after stripping HTML comments: the file explains the old palette, and the first version of that check flagged its own explanation). Two things only a browser settles, so `test-header.js` drives the page at 390 and 1280: the contrast a label actually renders at, and whether a rule WON — `theme.css` hides `.product` below 560px, which is right in the app where the tabs name the screen and wrong here where there are none, so `.brand .product` (0,2,0) has to beat the bare `.product` (0,1,0) inside that media query. Removing that one declaration renders the card as "DIGIT2AI" with no product name **on the phone only**, and the browser test fails at 390 while passing at 1280.

**THE CLAY THAT CARRIES WHITE TEXT IS DARKER THAN THE BRAND CLAY.** White on `--accent` (`#d97757`) measures **3.12:1** — under AA for a label this size — so every filled control in the app was failing it, and the login's old purple button was in fact *better* (6.52:1). `--accent` is still the brand and still paints borders, focus rings and washes; anything with white text on top now uses `--accent-solid` (`#b05537`, 5.00:1) with `--accent-solid-dark` for hover, which is darker rather than lighter. Three rules moved: `.btn.primary`, `.icon.send`, `.step.on`. The suite computes the ratio from the tokens rather than trusting the hex, and fails if one of those three goes back to the brand clay.

**EVERY VERSIONED ASSET IS CHECKED, NOT JUST `console.js`.** `theme.css` is now requested by three pages and the worker, so the `?v=` drift that once left `app.html` on v4 and `sw.js` on v3 has more places to happen; the offline suite walks `app.html`, `meetings.html`, `login.html` and `sw.js` and fails if any asset carries two versions, naming which file holds which. Changing `theme.css` means bumping its `?v=` in all four **and** the worker's `CACHE`.

**THE APP CARRIES THE COMPANY BRAND: DIGIT2AI** (owner request). The header leads with the DIGIT2AI lockup and the product name sits beside it behind a divider, dropping away below 560px where the tabs already say which screen you are on. The icon is **D2** — white with the brand cyan on the brand ink — because the full wordmark at 32px is a grey smear; a lone "2" was tried and is ambiguous, and a stacked DIGIT/2AI turns to mush. The lockup takes the page colour through `currentColor`, so ONE file reads on the paper interior and on the brand ink; only the 2 is fixed, because that cyan is the brand. Caveat recorded rather than hidden: the wordmark is an SVG text element in a Helvetica/Arial/Roboto stack, not outlines — there is no font-to-path tool here, so the letterforms shift slightly on Android. **The safe-zone guard is now MEASURED, not computed**: it renders the icon and finds the bounding box of every non-background pixel, because the old version derived the box from the shape's own coordinates and a glyph has none. **And `.top img` (0,1,1) beats a bare `.wordmark` (0,1,0) however late it appears** — the lockup rendered as a 22px square until the rule was qualified `.top img.wordmark`. That is invisible to a source grep, so `test-header.js` measures it in a browser and fails unless it is at least three times wider than tall. This REPLACED the S-that-speaks mark described below, which stays in the history but no longer ships.

**THE MARK IS AN S THAT SPEAKS, KNOCKED OUT OF A CLAY TILE** (`public/icon-master.svg`, regenerated by `node verticals/speakup/scripts/make-icons.js`; `--check` fails if an asset is stale). It replaced a purple gradient microphone on a near-black plate, wrong twice over: the palette belonged to the old theme, and a microphone describes half an app whose other half is a build pipeline. Drawn after looking at Claude, ChatGPT and Perplexity, whose marks share a grammar — ONE abstract shape, monochrome, flat, no letterform. This one keeps an S because the abstract alternatives were measurably worse when rendered: arcs-into-a-slab died at 32px (the faded strokes vanished), bars-and-a-triangle read as a media player, quotes-and-a-caret turned to noise. The two short rays off the open end are the only thing that says SPEECH rather than monogram. **Four rules in that script are load-bearing, not taste:** full-bleed square with no rounded corners in the source (iOS rounds `apple-touch-icon` itself; a pre-rounded source gets double-rounded), everything inside the central 80% so one file also works as `maskable` (the guard caught a ray Android would have clipped), no gradient and no opacity, and the curve COMPUTED from two circles rather than a hand-written bezier — the hand-written one collapsed into a lumpy ring, visible only by actually rendering it. Every candidate was judged at 512 AND rendered at 32 and blown back up.

**THE HEADER CONTROLS ARE ONE NODE, AND THE BURGER IS THE MENU AT EVERY WIDTH** (owner request
2026-09-21; it used to be a row of links in the bar above 700px and a panel below it — two shapes
for one set of controls). `#hdrMenu` is always the panel and `.burger` is always shown. Duplicating the markup is how a drawer and a bar end up offering different things, so the suite fails if either page carries a second `#langBtn` or `#outBtn`. The behaviour is shared too (`public/header-menu.js`): it closes on Escape, on an outside tap, on choosing anything, and **when the window grows past the breakpoint** — without that last one an X-shaped burger is stranded on a desktop with nothing to close. `node verticals/speakup/test-header.js` drives it in a real browser on BOTH screens at 360/390/1280 (66 checks: the panel opens, stays on screen, paints above the page, 44px targets, every close path) and SKIPS LOUDLY without puppeteer rather than reporting a pass it did not earn.

**WHISPER'S REPETITION LOOPS ARE STOPPED WHILE THEY FORM, AND COLLAPSED IF THEY GET THROUGH** (owner report 2026-09-17). A real meeting transcript came back **88% loop** — one phrase repeated 86 times in a row, another 65 times — each about one 30-second window decoded all the way to Whisper's **448-token ceiling**. Whisper writes every word with the previous ones as context, and on unclear audio it locks onto a phrase. OpenAI's reference implementation catches this by re-decoding any window whose text compresses above **2.4** (gzip ratio); the loops measured **28-32** and the real speech **1.42**. **transformers.js implements none of those checks** — `compression_ratio_threshold`, `logprob_threshold`, `no_speech_threshold` are absent from 3.3.3 — so nothing stopped them. Two layers now:
- **`no_repeat_ngram_size: 8`** on the pipeline call. Traced through the library source rather than assumed: the pipeline spreads its options into `generation_config`, and Whisper's `generate()` hands that to `_get_logits_processor`, which adds `NoRepeatNGramLogitsProcessor`. 8 and not smaller, because a small n also forbids legitimate repeats inside ordinary speech, while any n breaks a loop within one period plus n tokens. **`repetition_penalty` is deliberately NOT used** — it taxes every word already said, "the" and "and" included.
- **`public/transcript-clean.js`** collapses any phrase repeated **3+ times in a row** to one copy (case- and punctuation-insensitive, shortest repeating unit wins, idempotent, the output is always a subsequence of the input so nothing is invented or reordered; **two copies are left alone**, because people do repeat a sentence). **ONE FILE, TWO CALLERS:** the meetings page loads it before `record-engine.js`, and `src/routes/recordings.js` requires the same file and cleans on every server write — create, autosave (cleaned **before** the 800,000-character cap, so a loop cannot spend it and truncate the speech after it) and the server engine's output (the stub's labelled placeholder is left alone). The server pass exists because the phone is not the only writer and not always a current one: an installed app kept serving a cached recorder repeatedly this session, and every summary and extraction reads the stored text.

**What this does NOT fix, stated so it is not mistaken for more:** it cannot recover speech a loop replaced — that window was spent repeating instead of transcribing, and the audio never leaves the device, so a meeting already damaged stays damaged. It removes the loops; the accuracy ceiling is still the model. Phones run **`whisper-tiny`** (the smallest Whisper, the most loop-prone) and desktop `whisper-base`, both 8-bit, in a browser. A **silence gate was considered and rejected**: the owner records far-field on a phone in a room, and with no stored audio to calibrate a floor against, a floor set too high deletes quiet real speech — worse than the defect. **The fixture in the suite is invented**, because the repository is public: it reproduces the shape that failed without any meeting wording.

**The recording engine was RELOCATED, NOT REWRITTEN** — `public/record-engine.js` (`window.SpeakUpRecorder`) holds the mic + `getDisplayMedia` Web Audio mix, the rolling `MediaRecorder` segments (**90 s**, which is what the code always did, despite the "2.5 minutes" the old note claimed), on-device Whisper, per-segment autosave, the `localStorage` mirror, wake lock and crash recovery, byte-faithful; only its DOM writes became events.

A security review of the rebuild found five things worth keeping fixed. **The plan hash is now part of the atomic update** (`transition({expectPlanHash})`), not a read-then-write — a revision landing between the read and the write satisfied the status predicate again, so GitHub could have received a plan the owner never read. **A revision refreshes the registry snapshot** (`repo`, `base_branch`, `workflow_file`, `test_commands`, `path_scope`): the new hash attests the live project row, so leaving the old snapshot made the approved hash and what actually runs disagree about scope and tests. **A failed revision no longer terminally fails the job** — `FAILED` has no way back and `llm.callJSON` deliberately does not retry a credit or rate-limit error, so one 429 used to destroy a plan the owner had already read and corrected; the previous plan is restored and the error reported. **Corrections are capped** (`SPEAKUP_MAX_REVISIONS`, 8) because every one is re-sent in each later prompt. **A dead request re-syncs**: the console re-fetches on a failed revision rather than leaving a stale plan collecting further corrections (the revise call runs the plan model inside the request, and this origin sits behind a ~100 s proxy ceiling). One review finding was wrong and is worth recording as such: `src/services/ai-editor.js` is NOT dead code — `intents.js` still uses it for the read-only summary path.

New column: `su_jobs.revisions` JSONB (idempotent ALTER in `index.js`, canonical migration kept in step). New endpoints: `POST /factory/jobs/:id/approve`, `POST /factory/jobs/:id/revise`, `POST /factory/recordings/:id/prompt` — all operator-only and same-origin.

### Claude Code — the THIRD tab: ONE BOX, and the conversation is the unit

`/speakup/claude-code`, beside Reuniones and Fábrica. **One page, one box.** You type, it works,
the answer appears under your message, you type again — and a follow-up continues the SAME branch
and the SAME pull request. Behind each message: clone -> branch -> code -> install -> test ->
commit -> push -> pull request, streamed live.

**`/speakup/` IS THE BUILDER; THE FACTORY CONSOLE HAS NO DOOR** (owner request 2026-09-21, after
it kept appearing at sign-in). The root redirects, login lands there, the manifest starts there,
and the worker's offline fallback names it. **The page file stays, unreachable, and that is not
laziness:** `src/factory/security` (the operator gate, the same-origin check, the rate limiter),
`audit`, `jobs.readiness` and `intents` are what the Builder and the Meetings screen are BUILT ON,
and `test-offline.js` guards them through assertions that read `console.js`. Deleting the file
means rewriting that suite — a separate, careful job, not a side effect of hiding a page.

**THE TAB BAR IS `Reuniones | Code`, AND THE INSTALLED APP OPENS ON CODE** (owner request
2026-09-21). The Factory link is gone from every screen and the manifest's `start_url` moved;
the console is still served at `/speakup/` and `test-console-flow.js` still drives it — a working
subsystem is UNLINKED, never deleted. Removing the link broke four screens' language toggle,
because each relabelled `$('tabFac').textContent` and a missing node threw, killing the whole
relabel function: tabs are now set through a null-safe helper.

**IT HAS A WORKING FOLDER, WHICH IS WHY IT USED TO FEEL NOTHING LIKE THE DESKTOP APP.** Claude
Code on a Mac opens a directory that is already cloned and already installed; this cloned from
scratch every turn and deleted the result, so every code change paid for a full clone and a full
`npm ci` — minutes, on a repository this size. `repoDirFor()` gives each repository a folder that
STAYS: `git fetch` + `reset --hard` + `clean` (excluding `node_modules`), and a `lockStamp()` that
reinstalls only when the lockfile actually moved, stamped only after a SUCCESSFUL install. The
folder is shared, so a cancel and the restart sweep remove only the run's private HOME and leave
it to be repaired; `withFolder()` serialises turns per folder so two conversations on one
repository cannot share a checkout, while unrelated repositories still run side by side.

**IT TYPES.** `includePartialMessages: true` — without it the SDK only yields a FINISHED assistant
message, so the screen sat on a moving bar and then printed two minutes of work at once, which was
the single biggest reason this read as a batch job rather than a conversation. A delta is
`store.push()`, which reaches the bus and never the table: persisting them would put thousands of
rows in `cc_run_events` per run to rebuild a message the finished event already carries whole. The
page grows the bubble from the deltas and the stored summary replaces it when the turn ends.

**THE FIRST VERSION WAS A FORM, A CARD AND A SEPARATE RUN PAGE, AND THE OWNER COULD NOT USE IT**
("this should be like Claude, plain and simple" — 2026-09-21, after a run left them on a screen
with no input box and a Back link). The unit is now the CONVERSATION, not the job: `cc_threads`
holds the repository, the branch, the pull request and the agent session, and each turn is a
`cc_runs` row belonging to it, so a thread needs no message table. The first turn creates the
branch; every later turn fetches and checks out that same one, reuses the pull request rather than
opening a second, and **carries the earlier turns in its prompt** — a follow-up that cannot see
what came before is not a follow-up. One turn at a time per conversation; a second message while
the first works is refused plainly, the way a chat waits. The repository picker is on screen only
until the first message. `/claude-code/runs/:id` redirects to the conversation that run belongs
to, so an old link still lands somewhere true. Operator only, the SAME allow-list the
Factory uses — a run clones a repository, spends money and pushes a branch, so it is the
Factory's authority reached a different way and does not get a second, weaker gate. Code:
`src/claudecode/{runner,store,github,redact}.js` + `src/routes/claude-code.js` +
`public/claude-code.html|.js|.css`. Tables `cc_threads`, `cc_runs`, `cc_run_events`, `cc_repos`
(canonical `migrations/20260920_claude_code.sql`). Full runbook:
`verticals/speakup/README-claude-code.md`.

**THE ENGINE IS `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` `query()`, NEVER THE MESSAGES API.** The work
is "change this repository until the tests pass" — a tool loop over a filesystem — and the SDK
already owns the loop, the tool definitions, the session (so a failing test is handed back with
`resume` rather than restarting the reasoning) and the cost accounting. Against the Messages API
all four would be re-implemented, and the cost figure would become an estimate.

**IT IS LOADED WITH `import()`, NOT `require()`, AND THAT IS NOT STYLE.** The package is ESM
(`"type":"module"`); `require()` of ESM only works from Node 22.12 onward, and this repository's
`engines` says `>=16` with no pin on Render — a `require()` here works on a laptop and throws
`ERR_REQUIRE_ESM` in production. It is also lazy, so the vertical boots and the SIT runs with the
package absent, and an absent package is reported as itself instead of crashing the app.

**THE PEER CONFLICT IS SCOPED IN `overrides`, NOT IN `.npmrc`.** The agent SDK declares a peer on
`@anthropic-ai/sdk >= 0.93` while this repository pins `^0.67` for about forty call sites across
the verticals. That peer is **declarative only** — `sdk.mjs` is fully bundled and imports nothing
from it at runtime, verified by loading it with no peers installed at all. A root
`legacy-peer-deps=true` was the first answer and was wrong: it excuses EVERY peer conflict in the
repository and in CI for ever, so the next dependency with a real peer requirement would install
silently unsatisfied and fail at runtime instead of at install time. `package.json` `overrides`
pins the exemption to this one package. Revisit if the agent SDK ever starts importing it.

**A CLONED REPOSITORY MAY INSTRUCT; IT MAY NOT EXECUTE.** `settingSources:['project']` is what
makes the repository's own `CLAUDE.md` and skills outrank the agent's habits — the feature. The
same source also carries `.claude/settings.json`, which can declare `hooks`, and `.mcp.json`:
either runs a shell command on this production server before the first model turn, and anyone
with push access to any repository the allow-list permits could plant one. `disarmWorkspace()`
deletes both from the workspace after the clone and before the agent starts. CLAUDE.md, the
skills and the code are untouched.

**THE AGENT NEVER SEES THIS SERVER'S ENVIRONMENT, AND THE SDK MAKES THAT AN ACTIVE CHOICE.** Its
own documentation: an omitted `env` INHERITS `process.env`, a supplied one REPLACES it. So it is
supplied — `PATH`, a per-run private `HOME`, `LANG`, `TERM`, `CI` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and
nothing else. Otherwise one `bash -c 'env'` inside a bypassPermissions session hands a repository
`DATABASE_URL` (the live CRM Postgres), `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET`
and the rest, and a `curl` carrying them away never passes through redaction, which only guards
what is STORED. The cloned repository's own `npm test` runs under the same allow-list.

**THE PUSH CREDENTIAL DOES NOT LIVE IN THE CLONE.** `git clone https://x-access-token:<token>@…`
writes that URL verbatim into `.git/config`, and the agent has Bash in that directory — one `cat`
would read an org-wide write token, with no environment access at all, which is why this survives
the env allow-list. The remote is rewritten to a plain URL immediately after the clone; the push
supplies the credential explicitly.

**NOTHING CREDENTIAL-SHAPED IS COMMITTED TO A PUBLIC REPOSITORY.** The staged diff is scanned
before every commit and the run refuses rather than pushing; `.env`, `.git/` and key files are
never committed whatever the diff says. A sentence in the prompt was the only control before, and
a sentence is not a control.

**THE CONFIGURATION GATE: THIS SURFACE MUST NOT BE LOOSER THAN THE FACTORY.** While
`SPEAKUP_TEAM_PASSWORD` is the value this public repository publishes, or `SPEAKUP_JWT_SECRET` is
unset, the run / intake / merge routes answer **423** and name the fix — reading past runs still
works. Without it, anyone who had read the repo could sign in, be an operator by email, and reach
a surface that executes code, while the Factory next door refused them for exactly that reason.
The role and the email are also re-read from the DATABASE on every request, never trusted from a
30-day token, so an account demoted today loses this surface today.

**A CANCEL IS FINAL.** `store.setStatus` is a compare-and-swap that refuses to leave a terminal
status and reports whether the row moved. Before it, a cancel landing between a check and the
next write was simply overwritten — cancelled -> running, so the operator who pressed Cancel was
shown "Failed"; and in the push window, cancelled -> pushing -> pr_open, which with auto-merge on
reached main. The cancel also kills the live `git`/`npm` PROCESS GROUP rather than leaving a
twenty-minute test to its own timeout while it holds the slot and the workspace.

**A DOCUMENTATION-ONLY CHANGE INSTALLS NOTHING AND RUNS NOTHING.** Asking a question about a
large repository usually ends in a one-line README edit, and the owner watched a docs answer sit
for minutes at `npm ci` on this repo proving that a `.md` file passes `jest`. The staged diff is
inspected first; a change confined to markdown, text and `docs/` is reported NOT MEASURED with the
reason — never as a pass, because the run does not get to claim a suite it never ran.

**A FRESH CLONE INSTALLS BEFORE IT TESTS, AND A FAILED INSTALL IS NOT A RED SUITE.** `npm test` in
a shallow clone of this repository is `jest`, which exits 127 in a tree that has never been
installed — reported as a FAILING SUITE, that fed "the tests failed, fix it" to the agent three
times, paid for each pass, and opened a draft PR the merge button then refused. Every run against
any repository with devDependencies ended red. Now `npm ci --ignore-scripts` runs first (a
lockfile's postinstall is arbitrary code from the cloned repository) and an install that cannot
succeed reports **not measured**, which is what stops the fix loop.

**NOTHING IS LEFT RUNNING BY A RESTART.** A boot sweep fails every non-terminal run with
"interrupted by a server restart" and removes its workspace. A run cannot outlive the process
that was running it, so anything non-terminal at startup was cut off by definition — without the
sweep the row stayed `running` for ever, every watching browser held a ping interval and a bus
listener, and the duration ticked up without bound.

**THE MONEY AND THE TOKENS ARE COPIED, NEVER COMPUTED.** `cost_usd`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out` and
`turns` come from the SDK's own `result` message. A run that never reaches one stores `null` — an
honest "not measured" rather than a plausible number — and the SIT greps the runner to prove no
figure is derived from a token count times a rate. **A measured ZERO is not an absence**: `|| null`
turned a subscription-billed run's real `0` into "not measured", so only `null` may mean that.

**A TEST PASS IS MEASURED OR IT IS NOT CLAIMED.** The repository's own `npm test` runs when its
`package.json` declares one; otherwise the run says plainly that nothing was measured. It never
invents a command, and a red suite opens a DRAFT pull request that the merge button refuses.

**WRITE ACCESS IS CHECKED BEFORE A RUN STARTS, NOT DISCOVERED AT THE PUSH.** The first live run
cloned, ran twenty turns, spent $0.23 and wrote a commit — then died on `Permission to
digit2ai/CRM-Co-Pilot.git denied`. Everything before the push only needs READ, and the fallback
credential is the Factory's fine-grained PAT, which is scoped to ONE repository, so every other
repo failed at the last step after the money was gone. `github.canPush()` asks GitHub at creation
time; a refusal is a 403 naming the fix, nothing is stored, and `cc_repos.can_push` badges the
picker. An unreachable GitHub refuses rather than assuming write access.

**THE OWNER ALLOW-LIST IS ENFORCED AT THE SERVER, AT EVERY STEP.** `github.assertAllowed()` guards
the clone, the pull request and the merge, not only the create route: a run is a disposable VM
holding a write token, and "the page only offers our repositories" is a statement about a page.
`GITHUB_ORG` names the owners; unset it falls back to the owner of `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_REPO`, never to
anyone. A malformed or traversal name is refused before it can reach a clone path.

**SECRETS NEVER REACH AN EVENT.** `redact.js` scrubs on the way IN — every event is persisted to
`cc_run_events` and pushed down an SSE stream, so a value scrubbed only at display time is still a
value sitting in the database for ever. It works by VALUE (the tokens this process holds, including
the one inside the clone URL) and by SHAPE (Anthropic, GitHub, AWS and Stripe credential prefixes),
so a key belonging to someone else — pasted into a repo, printed by a test — is caught too.

**THE WORKSPACE IS DISPOSABLE, WHICH IS WHAT LICENSES `bypassPermissions`.** A fresh shallow clone
under `CC_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, removed on every terminal status including a crash, holding one
credential for a repository the allow-list already approved. Git is spawned with an argv and never a
shell string, so a branch name derived from a brief can never become a command. Ceilings: **3
concurrent runs per tenant**, **$10 per run**, 200 turns, 3 test-fix cycles — whichever trips first
stops the run and names itself.

**A REPOSITORY WITHOUT THE HOUSE SKILL GETS OURS COPIED IN**, into the throwaway workspace only, so
the run follows the same conventions as everything else here; the page badges which case a
repository is in. `settingSources:['project']` is what makes the repository's own `CLAUDE.md` and
skills outrank the agent's habits.

**THE SPEAKUP TRANSFER NOW OPENS A CLAUDE CODE RUN** (`source='speakup'`,
`source_ref='meeting:<id>'`), and the chat shows the run link. Everything that guarded the old path
still runs first — the operator check, the rate limit, the duplicate guard, the model-required
conversion and `meetingLeaks()`. **The human gate MOVED rather than disappearing**: from "type
approved before it builds" to "merge the pull request before it ships", which is where it has to be,
because a run ends at a branch and a PR and never at `main`. `SPEAKUP_TRANSFER_ENGINE=factory`
restores the old path; `meetings.js` renders a `cc:<id>` reference as its own link.

**THE HEADER TAKES A SECOND ROW ON A PHONE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED.** Three tabs beside the lockup
laid out at **417px** at 360, 390 and 414 — it did not overflow visibly because `app.html` sets
`body{overflow:hidden}`, and the only thing that noticed was the install bar, which `test-pwa.js`
found sitting off the right edge. Truncating a tab label or shrinking the brand mark both cost more
than a second row, so below 560px `.tabs` takes one (`order:1;flex:1 0 100%`). The drawer is
positioned at `top:100%` of the header, so it follows the taller bar by itself.

**Endpoints** (`/speakup/api/v1/claude-code`, operator + `X-SpeakUp: 1` + same-host Origin on every
mutation): `GET /config` · `GET /repos`, `POST /repos/:id/sync`, `POST /repos/refresh` ·
`POST /runs`, `GET /runs`, `GET /runs/:id`, `GET /runs/:id/stream` (SSE, replays stored events then
follows the bus), `POST /runs/:id/cancel|merge` · `POST /intake` (the door SpeakUp and the Factory
use — same gates, same creation path).

**AUTO-MERGE FAILS SHUT, AND THAT WORD IS LOAD-BEARING.** `combinedStatus` used to return
`unknown` both when GitHub reported nothing AND when the API call itself failed, and the merge
treated `unknown` as permission — so one transient 502 squash-merged unreviewed agent output into
a public main and fired the deploy hook. The two are now different values (`error` vs `none`), it
reads **check-runs as well as the legacy statuses** (a repository whose CI is Actions reports
nothing through statuses alone, so reading statuses only would have refused on exactly the
repositories this is for), and a merge additionally needs a green MEASURED suite. A run
transferred from a meeting is never auto-merged whatever the setting, and neither is one the agent
itself reported an error on. **The deploy hook belongs to ONE repository** (`CC_DEPLOY_REPO`):
firing it after merging a PR elsewhere would redeploy this CRM from another repo's main.

**THE CONCURRENCY CEILING IS CLAIMED SYNCHRONOUSLY.** `atCapacity` alone was checked about three
awaits before the runner registered the run, so a burst all saw an empty table and all started —
N clones on the shared instance's `/tmp` and N times the cost cap. `reserve()` runs before the
first `await` and the runner hands the slot back as it registers.

**SIT:** `node verticals/speakup/sit-claude-code.js` -> **305/305**, zero external keys and no
database: a fake `query()` stands in for the SDK, a fake GitHub answers REST, and the three tables
are held in memory. It attacks the invariants — a secret in a tool result reaching an event, a
repository outside the allow-list, a cost derived instead of copied, a test pass claimed without a
suite, a `tenant_id` in a body being honoured, a cross-tenant read, a draft PR being merged, a
fourth concurrent run — and, since the 2026-09-20 reviews, every fix above: a child given the
allow-list cannot see an unrelated variable, a planted hooks file is gone before the agent starts
while CLAUDE.md survives, an unreachable GitHub reads as `error`, a cancelled run cannot reach
`pr_open`, a published team password closes the door with 423, a demotion in the database takes
effect on the next request, and an install that cannot succeed is not-measured rather than red.
**NOT covered:** a real Agent SDK run, the real GitHub API, a real clone and push, the Postgres
store, and whether Render's runtime user permits `bypassPermissions` — verify with `GET /config`
on the deploy, then one real brief.

**Environment Variables:** `GITHUB_TOKEN` (falls back to `CC_GITHUB_TOKEN`, then
`SPEAKUP_GITHUB_TOKEN`; unset = cloning CLOSED) · `GITHUB_ORG` (owners a run may touch; unset =
the owner of `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_REPO`) · `CC_WORKSPACE_ROOT` (`/tmp/cc-workspaces`) · `CC_MODEL`
(`claude-sonnet-5`) · `CC_MAX_TURNS` (200) · `CC_AUTO_MERGE` (`false`) · `RENDER_DEPLOY_HOOK_URL`
(unset = the run stops at `merged`) · `CC_COST_CAP_USD` (10) · `CC_MAX_CONCURRENT` (3) ·
`CC_MAX_FIX_CYCLES` (3) · `CC_DEFAULT_REPO` · `CC_DEPLOY_REPO` (the one repository whose merge
fires the deploy hook) · `CC_DEPLOY_URL` · `CC_EVENTS_PAGE` (500) · `SPEAKUP_TRANSFER_ENGINE`
(`claude-code`) · reuses `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_ALLOWED_EMAILS`, and requires
`SPEAKUP_TEAM_PASSWORD` + `SPEAKUP_JWT_SECRET` to be set before a run may start.

### SpeakUp AI Factory — the iPhone as the front end of RinglyPro Architect

SpeakUp is also the phone interface to the AI development workflow: **speak, review, approve, check status.** Voice becomes a note, a meeting or a command; a meeting becomes classified intelligence; approved requirements become a plan checked against the deployed code; an approved plan runs **Claude Code in GitHub Actions** on a review branch and SpeakUp opens the PR. Merge is a separate, gated step, and Render deploys main exactly as before. Code: `verticals/speakup/src/factory/*`, `src/routes/factory.js`, `public/factory.js`, `.github/workflows/speakup-factory.yml`, `.github/speakup/*.js`. Tables `su_projects, su_meeting_intel, su_commands, su_jobs, su_audit` + `mode/project_key/participants` on `su_recordings` (canonical `migrations/20260915_speakup_factory.sql`).

**THE APP IS ONE CONSOLE: 80% WORK ON TOP, 20% INSTRUCTION BOX AT THE BOTTOM** (`public/app.html` + `public/console.js`). No modes, no project picker, no approval tap: an instruction goes to **digit2ai/RinglyPro-CRM** (project `ringlypro`, whole-repo scope) and the result is always a branch and a PR, never main. The top pane is the terminal of the running job — statuses, then every file Claude reads, edits (expandable to the old and new lines), runs and tests, then the PR link and the diff on demand; the bottom is a monospace box with the mic inside it. The owner asked for exactly this after the tabbed version; the earlier screen (meetings, notes, library, import, the orb) is preserved at **`/speakup/recorder`** (`public/recorder.html` + `public/factory.js`) and still works.

**A FINISHED JOB IS DISMISSED FOR GOOD, A RUNNING ONE ALWAYS COMES BACK.** On boot the console re-attached to the newest job whatever its state, so one completed run reappeared in every new window with no way to get rid of it. Now a **Clear** control in the step bar empties the pane and records the job id in `localStorage.speakup_console_dismissed`; boot restores a job that is still running (close the tab, come back, the work is on screen) and a finished one only when it is newer than the dismissed id. Clearing NEVER cancels — the job keeps running on GitHub. **`app.html` and `sw.js` must request the same `console.js?v=N`**: they had drifted to v4 and v3, so the installed app kept serving the cached file and a deployed fix looked like it had never shipped. The offline suite fails if they disagree.

**THERE IS NO EMPTY PAGE, AND CLEAR ONLY EVER CLEARS A FINISHED JOB** (owner request 2026-09-17). The owner kept landing on five grey steps with none active, a Clear button with nothing to clear, and a dark pane holding one sentence — *while a plan was still waiting for them on the server*. The cause was Clear: it was drawn **unconditionally**, including beside Cancel on a live plan, and there it did two bad things. It hid the plan, which is the empty page; and it nulled `planJob`, so the next message skipped the correction path and **started a second job underneath the unread plan**. "No new job under a plan waiting to be read" is enforced only in `console.js` — the server does not refuse a second PREPARE — and Clear was the way around it. Now Clear is drawn only for a terminal job, `clearPane()` returns early on a live one (a live job has Cancel), and there is no idle paragraph at all: with nothing in progress `body.idle` hides the step bar and the work pane, so the screen is the header and the instruction box. Every door into work — `write()`, `showPlan()`, `renderBar(job)` — calls `leaveIdle()`, or a job would render into a hidden pane. Startup already landed on a waiting plan (`TERMINAL` is only DEPLOYED/FAILED/CANCELLED, and a restore does set `planJob`), so after this the owner goes straight to the plan whenever there is one. **Worth knowing, not fixed:** the server-side rule is still missing, so a stale cached client could still start a second job. `node verticals/speakup/test-console-flow.js` → **30/30** drives five scenarios at 390 and 1280 (nothing in progress, a waiting plan, a correction on it firing **zero** `/command` calls, clearing a finished job, and leaving idle on send); mutation-tested by restoring the unconditional button, which fails four checks.

**ONE POLLER, AND THE END IS ANNOUNCED ONCE** (seen live 2026-09-17: the same pull request link printed seven times and the bar kept sliding after "Deployed"). `visibilitychange` calls `follow()`, so every return to the tab started ANOTHER polling loop beside the running one, and each of them printed the closing line. A generation number retires the older loop at its next await, `announced[id]` keeps the closing lines to one per job, and the terminal branch clears the working state explicitly instead of relying on the next poll that a stopped loop never makes.

**AND THE BAR CARRIES A LIVE WORD AND A CLOCK** (owner request 2026-09-17). A sliding bar on its own could be a stuck animation, and the owner needs to know not to close the app or type over a running job. Beside it sits a gerund that changes every four seconds — Musing, Percolating, Shimmying, Forging… (`WORK_WORDS`, 30 English and 25 Spanish, drawn at random and never the same one twice in a row) — plus the elapsed time counting up in seconds, then minutes. A `beforeunload` guard asks before the tab is closed while work is in flight (the job keeps running on GitHub either way; this stops it being lost by accident). Both stop the moment the box is usable again. The browser test waits out a real rotation and asserts the clock moved AND the word changed.

**WHILE SOMETHING IS RUNNING, THE SEND BUTTON GIVES ITS PLACE TO A MOVING BAR** (owner request 2026-09-17). Through planning, coding and an answer being written the button sat there enabled, with no sign that anything was happening. `setWorking()` hides and disables it and shows `#prog` instead; the button returns the moment the box is usable. **A plan waiting for a word is NOT "working"** — `approved` and a correction are typed into that same box, so `showPlan()` and the poller's `WAITING_APPROVAL` branch hand it straight back. `prefers-reduced-motion` gets a static bar. Covered in the browser by `test-console-flow.js` (mutation-tested: dropping the poller's call fails four checks).

**SCREENSHOTS ARE PASTED INTO THE BOX** (`su_uploads`, `su_jobs.attachments`). Cmd+V or drag an image into the console: it is stored in the database (Render disk is ephemeral), travels with the job, and the build job downloads it into the runner temp directory with its progress token, where the brief names the paths and tells Claude to read them before changing anything. Images only (PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF, 6 MB, six per job), operator only, served as a download with `nosniff`, and never written into the repository. An upload not yet attached to a job cannot be fetched by a runner at all.

**AN INFORMATION REQUEST IS NOT A COMMAND, AND A COMMAND WORD IS NOT A COMMAND.** Three routing bugs, each one found by typing the phrasing rather than reasoning about it: "give me a summary of the auto merge" matched the APPROVE_MERGE rule and came back as a confirmation card for putting code into production; "add a summary line to the header" matched the SUMMARIZE rule and went looking for a meeting instead of building anything; "is it deployed" and "update me on the status" were read as requests to deploy and to update. The rule is now three ordered checks in `isQuestion`, and the order IS the fix: an information cue wins outright (`INFO_CUE`/`INFO_ASK` — "tell me", "update me", and "give me" only in front of an information noun, so "give me a Clear button" still builds), then a command verb in its **base form** makes it an instruction however politely phrased (`isInstruction` -> PREPARE, which is what stops a rule word in the middle of a sentence hijacking it), and only then do a question mark or an opening question word decide. Base forms only: the -ed/-ing forms describe a state or a hypothesis, which is why "is it deployed" was being read as a command. A question resolves against the READ-ONLY intents through `classifyRulesAmong`, never the full registry. `SUMMARIZE` falls back to `ASK` when nothing recorded matches — unless the text NAMES a conversation (`refersToConversation`), where answering from the code would quietly substitute a different subject for the one asked about.

**THE FACTORY TALKS LIKE CLAUDE: ASK ANYTHING, ANSWERED LIVE, ON THE SUBSCRIPTION** (owner request 2026-09-17). "Search for…", "investigate…", "get into this repo and summarize it", "what was the latest commit" used to go to keyword excerpts sent to an API account with no credit, so they came back empty. Now:
- **Every model call prefers the Claude subscription** (`llm.useSubscription()`): plans, intel, answers and intent all run through the pinned Claude Code CLI when `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is set, so plans are no longer `heuristic`. Suites that inject a fake client unset the token first (`sit-factory`, `sit-meeting-chat`).
- **Questions and find-out requests go to a READ-ONLY research agent** (`src/factory/research.js`, `claude-subscription.research()`), streamed by `POST /factory/research` (operator, same-origin, 30 / 10 min, NDJSON: `tool` lines as it reads and searches, `delta`, `done`, a 15 s ping, killed when the tab closes). `/command` answers `client_action:'research'` for ASK and for ARCHITECT_REVIEW not tied to a meeting, and the console opens the stream, keeping the last 8 turns so follow-ups have context. `RESEARCH_CUE` (search, investigate, look up, check, review, busca, investiga, revisa…) makes a request a question ONLY after the change-verb test, so "investigate the login and fix it" still builds; SEARCH_MEMORY is used only when the text names a meeting, recording or note.
- **THE LOCKS, VERIFIED AGAINST THE REAL CLI, NOT ASSUMED:** `--restricted` confines the file tools to the checkout (reading `/etc/hosts` was refused as "outside the working directory" — this is what keeps `/proc/<pid>/environ`, every Render secret, out of reach) and removes Bash; `--tools Read,Grep,Glob,WebSearch,WebFetch` (no Edit/Write); `--permission-mode dontAsk` refuses anything not pre-approved; WebFetch is pre-approved only per domain in `FETCH_DOMAINS` (GitHub, api.github.com, docs sites — platforms where a stranger cannot read request logs, so an injected page cannot exfiltrate what was read; example.com was refused); `.git/**` and `.env` are denied on top (both verified refused for Read and Grep); env is the chat's allow-list. It can look, never touch: a change still goes through a plan and "approved".
- `SPEAKUP_RESEARCH_MODEL` (`claude-sonnet-5`) · `SPEAKUP_RESEARCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (300000).

**A QUESTION IS ANSWERED, NOT BUILT** (`intents.isQuestion` -> the `ASK` intent). Architect mode used to send every message to PREPARE, so "how does the merge gate work?" opened a code job. Now a question is answered read-only from the deployed checkout -- candidate files by keyword, bounded excerpts, one model call -- with no job, no branch and no write; the console prints it under ANSWER. Three rules keep the line where it belongs: **an instruction phrased politely is still an instruction** (a change verb anywhere disqualifies a question, so "can you add a TEST tag?" still builds), a pasted `/command` is never a question however it reads, and **a question may only reach a READ-ONLY intent** (`QUESTION_INTENTS`) -- without that list "how does the merge gate work?" matched the APPROVE_MERGE rule and came back as a confirmation card for putting code into production. Half the change verbs are also nouns, so a determiner in front ("the change", "the deploy") marks them as nouns before the test. **And a change verb in the PAST is a report, not an instruction** (`PAST_REPORT`): "why was no code changed?" and "what did you change?" ask about a job that already finished, but they carry a change verb, so they failed the test and opened a build job that had nothing to edit and died on `make-patch`'s "Claude produced no file changes" -- the question answered itself by reproducing the defect. Only auxiliaries that can ONLY refer to the past strip the verb (`was/were/did/had/been`), so "can the header be changed?" keeps its infinitive and still builds, and an imperative after a past clause still survives. An answer that cites a file which does not exist in the checkout is DISCARDED, not shown with a caveat.

**THE WAKE WORD ALONE IS A GREETING, NOT A JOB.** `/ringlypro-architect`, "ringlypro architect", "architect" or "arquitecto" with nothing after it answers with a READY line — **naming no repository** (the console only ever talks to one, so printing it on every wake was noise; owner request 2026-09-17) and **reading `SPEAKUP_AUTO_RUN` rather than asserting a behaviour**: it used to promise the change "runs straight away", which stopped being true when auto-run went off by default and the plan step returned. The same words followed by an instruction are the instruction (`intents.isWakeOnly`). The console also renames the machine statuses into plain words and hides WAITING_APPROVAL, which it approves as it dispatches.

**AND IT MERGES ITSELF** (`jobs.autoMerge`, `SPEAKUP_AUTO_MERGE`, default on). A console job whose CI tests passed squash-merges its own PR, and Render deploys main as it always has: voice or typing reaches the live page with no stop in between — the workflow the owner already had in VS Code. TWO things still stop it, and only these: a change that **edited a test suite the factory runs** (its green result cannot vouch for itself) and a merge the GitHub token is not allowed to make (needs Contents: Read and write); both leave the PR open, say why in the console, and wait for a person. Merging from the phone by hand still asks for the private phrase.

**THE CONSOLE RUNS WITHOUT A SECOND TAP** (`jobs.autoDispatch`, `su_jobs.auto_run`). It is the SAME door as `approveAndDispatch` — operator allow-list, readiness, project permission, atomic `WAITING_APPROVAL -> QUEUED` — minus the phrase, because the owner typed the instruction into their own signed-in console seconds earlier and the outcome is still only a branch and a PR. The phrase is still required to MERGE, which is what reaches production. `SPEAKUP_AUTO_RUN=off` restores the approval step.

**A PASTED PROMPT IS AN INSTRUCTION, NOT A COMMAND TO CLASSIFY.** In Architect mode anything long, multi-line or starting with a slash (`/ringlypro-architect ...`) goes straight to PREPARE and reaches Claude **word for word** (`spec.instruction`, up to 50k chars, in the brief under "THE OWNER INSTRUCTION, WORD FOR WORD"). The old path put it through the command rules — a prompt mentioning "status" or "deploy" was read as a status question — and truncated it to the 600-char item label. A direct instruction is also **excluded from the push guard's protected phrases**: it is the owner's own text for this repository, and guarding it would refuse every push.

**LIVE ACTIVITY** (`su_job_events`): `claude.js` runs with `--output-format stream-json`, `.github/speakup/stream-events.js` turns each event into one short line (paths made relative, text clipped, nothing printed to the public Actions log) and posts batches every 2.5 s to `POST /factory/progress-log`, authenticated by the **progress token** and refused unless the job is running and the plan hash matches. Kinds are an allow-list; an unknown kind is filed as `info`. The phone polls `GET /factory/jobs/:id/events?after=<id>`. Server-side transitions write their own `status` events, so the pane shows the whole life of a job.

**Modes:** Reunión (the default, so tapping the orb still records as SpeakUp always did: the existing segmented Whisper recorder, now with pause/resume, screen wake lock and a flush when the page is hidden), Comando (interpreted), Nota (always saved as a note, never interpreted, never touches code), Arquitecto (an explicit engineering request: the owner's own words become a human-approved requirement and go straight to PREPARE). Commands dictate with the Web Speech API where the browser has it (on iPhone that is Apple's recognizer, not on-device Whisper) and fall back to on-device Whisper; the transcript is editable before sending and survives a refresh of the tab (`sessionStorage.speakup_cmd_draft`, NOT localStorage: a dictated draft can contain the spoken private phrase, so it is cleared when the tab closes and whenever a send is attempted).

**The command interpreter is a registry** (`factory/intents.js`): each intent is one entry `{name, operator, modelSafe, match, handler}`. Rules classify first; a model may only choose among `modelSafe` intents and its answer is enum-gated. Intents: CAPTURE_NOTE · CAPTURE_MEETING · SEARCH_MEMORY · SUMMARIZE · EXTRACT_REQUIREMENTS ("analyze") · CREATE_BRD · CREATE_DEVELOPMENT_PROMPT · ARCHITECT_REVIEW (read-only, candidate files + labelled hypotheses) · PREPARE_IMPLEMENTATION · EXECUTE_IMPLEMENTATION · APPROVE_MERGE · CHECK_EXECUTION · CHECK_DEPLOYMENT · CANCEL_EXECUTION.

**A MEETING CAN NEVER TRIGGER A CODE CHANGE.** The recording, transcription and editing files do not import the factory (offline test greps them). EXECUTE, MERGE and CANCEL are never `modelSafe`, and their handlers return a **confirmation card**, never an action. The only door is `jobs.approveAndDispatch()` (and `jobs.merge()`), reached from `POST /factory/jobs/:id/execute|merge`, and it demands ALL of: an operator (role admin + `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_ALLOWED_EMAILS`, **role and email reloaded from the database on every factory request**), readiness (**SpeakUp's own `SPEAKUP_JWT_SECRET`, different from the shared `JWT_SECRET`**, **non-published team password**, a phrase of 4+ words / 20+ chars, factory secret, GitHub token), the **exact plan hash the phone displayed**, a job in WAITING_APPROVAL in the caller's tenant, the project's `allowed_actions` including `execute`, and the **private phrase** — typed, or spoken inside the command, which returns a 3-minute signed confirm token so one tap still confirms after the selection is shown. Single use is the atomic `WAITING_APPROVAL -> QUEUED` update. Any of those unset = the door is **closed and says which**, never open.

**THE PRIVATE PHRASE LIVES ONLY IN SERVER ENV** (`SPEAKUP_EXEC_PHRASE`, or `SPEAKUP_EXEC_PHRASE_SHA256` of the normalized phrase). It is detected in speech by hashing word windows, **redacted to `[frase privada]` before any transcript or command is stored**, and confirm tokens are never persisted. A spoken secret is heard by whoever is in the room; the plan-hash binding, the operator allow-list and the confirm tap are what keep that from being enough on its own. Only an operator's text is scanned for the phrase, every scan spends a budget (40 / 15 min), and the response never says whether it matched, so `/command` is not a guessing oracle.

**SESSION TOKENS CARRY `audience: 'speakup'`.** Several verticals fall back to the same `JWT_SECRET`, and exec-coaching's public student signup mints a token with a chosen `tenant_id`; without the audience check that token was a valid SpeakUp session. Tokens issued before 2026-09-15 have no audience, so everyone signs in once more.

**BRAINSTORMING NEVER BECOMES A REQUIREMENT** (`factory/intel.js`, verifier runs on model AND heuristic output): items are DISCUSSION | IDEA | SUGGESTION | DECISION | APPROVED_REQUIREMENT; every item needs a quote found in the transcript (accent/punctuation-insensitive) or it goes to `unverified`; APPROVED_REQUIREMENT stands only with an explicit approval cue in its quote ("queda aprobado", "let's do it"...) and confidence >= 0.5, or when **a human promoted it** in the intelligence view. PREPARE with zero approved requirements FAILS and states how many ideas/suggestions were held back.

**PREPARE READS; IT NEVER WRITES CODE** (`factory/prepare.js`, background job, polled). Candidate files come from the repository **this server was deployed from** (Render runs a checkout of main), by keyword match, labelled as candidates. A plan step may only name a file that exists, or a new file inside the project's `path_scope`; anything else moves to `unverified_files`. Uncovered requirements are listed. The plan hash covers sources, requirements and plan, so an edited plan cannot run under an old approval.

**THE REPOSITORY IS PUBLIC, SO THE FACTORY WRITES NOTHING PRIVATE TO GITHUB.** Branch `speakup/job-<id>`, commit message and PR title/body carry only the job number, project key, test counts and a sign-in link to the trace. Actions logs print no brief, plan, transcript or test output (all in `RUNNER_TEMP`). Claude is told not to copy meeting content into code or comments. SIT asserts the PR body carries no meeting words.

**THE CODE CLAUDE WRITES IS UNTRUSTED, SO THE WORKFLOW IS FOUR JOBS ON FOUR VMs** (`speakup-factory.yml`, `workflow_dispatch` only, `permissions: {}` by default). A security review found the first single-job version fatal: a job that runs Claude or the tests can use passwordless sudo, write git hooks or `core.hooksPath`, rewrite later steps' scripts or add to PATH, and so steal every secret and the push token that job holds — a prompt injection in a meeting quote would have been enough. Now:

| Job | Trust | Holds | Does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prepare` | trusted, runs no model/test/npm code | factory secret | fetches the brief with HMAC, reports CODING, mints a **single-use brief token** and a **progress token**, outputs the approved test commands and **hashes** of requirement text, quotes and participant names |
| `build` | UNTRUSTED | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, the two narrow tokens | Claude Code (`claude -p`, pinned version, allow-listed env, git commit/push/remote/config, curl, wget, gh, WebFetch, WebSearch denied), up to 2 fix rounds; output is a **patch (data)** |
| `verify` | UNTRUSTED | nothing | clean VM: applies the patch, runs the approved tests; output is test counts |
| `push` | trusted, runs no model/test/npm code | factory secret, `contents: write` token | applies the patch **as data** (refuses `.github/`, `.git/`, `.env`, `..`, symlinks, submodules, rename headers), refuses credential shapes and **any diff containing a hashed meeting quote shingle or participant name** (the repo is public), flags an edited test suite, commits and pushes `speakup/job-<id>` with **git hooks disabled** |
| `report-failure` | trusted | factory secret | reports FAILED with each job's result |

The progress token can only report TESTING, FIXING or a failure for its own job; the brief token works once and only while the job is CODING. Actions are pinned by commit SHA. A failing test in `verify` still pushes, as a draft PR with the job FAILED.

**EVERY PROJECT IS MEASURABLE, INCLUDING ONE CREATED TOMORROW** (owner request 2026-09-17). The fallback is a property of the REGISTRY, not of one project: `projects.commandsFor(project)` returns the project's own commands, or `DEFAULT_TEST_COMMANDS` (`node verticals/speakup/test-offline.js`) when its list is empty, and **prepare snapshots the job through it** — so a project added through `POST /projects` next month, with nothing configured, still runs a real suite and can still reach the merge gate. A project that names its own commands is never touched. The offline suite asserts the snapshot goes through `commandsFor` (bypassing it fails the build).

**A PROJECT WITH NO TEST COMMAND CAN NEVER PASS** (found live 2026-09-17). The console's own project, `ringlypro`, was seeded with `test_commands: []`, and `run-tests.js` only counts `node --check` on changed **.js** files — so the first change that added a static HTML page ran nothing at all, reported `measured:false`, and the merge gate (correctly) refused it: the job ended FAILED with a draft PR and the page never went live. `ringlypro` now carries `node verticals/speakup/test-offline.js` (keyless, DB-less, seconds), `ensureDefaults` **backfills** a seeded project whose commands are still empty (never overwriting one set by hand), and the offline suite fails if a default project has no command.

**WRITING TESTS FOR YOUR OWN CHANGE MUST NOT COST THE AUTOMATION** (owner 2026-09-17: "if this is not automatic, then it defeats the purpose of this app"). `suite_modified` blocked the automatic merge outright, and a good change usually DOES add tests — so the rule fired on the normal case and left every such change waiting for a person. The verify job now runs a **baseline pass**: each approved test script the change modified is restored to the base-branch version (`git checkout HEAD -- <path>`; the patch is applied but not committed, so HEAD is the base), the commands run again over the CHANGED product code, and the patched files are put back in a `finally`, so the pushed branch is exactly what Claude wrote. `baseline_ok` travels on the signed callback (a job dispatched before the field existed still verifies against the old field list) and is stored on `su_jobs.baseline_ok`. The gate is now: tests measured and green **and** (`suite_modified === false` **or** `baseline_ok === true`). A change that rewrites the suite to always pass while breaking the product still fails the baseline and still waits for a person — proved in the offline suite with a real git repo, and mutation-tested by deleting the restore.

**TEST COUNTS FROM A VM THAT RUNS THE CHANGE CAN BE FORGED BY THE CHANGE.** Code under test can print "999 passed". So the counts are recorded but **the phone merge gate refuses** when the change edited a script named by an approved test command (`suite_modified`), or touched a file outside the plan's files and the project's `path_scope`; the confirm card lists the changed files and says why. Those merges happen on GitHub after a person reads the diff.

**THE REGISTRY IS SNAPSHOTTED AT PREPARE.** `repo`, `base_branch`, `workflow_file`, `test_commands` and `path_scope` are copied into the job and covered by the plan hash; dispatch and the brief read the snapshot, never the live row, so editing a project between approval and execution cannot change what runs.

**Callbacks** (`POST /factory/callback`, no session): HMAC-SHA256 over a canonical array of 13 fields in fixed order (Express has consumed the raw body, so the body is never what is signed), 300 s timestamp window, nonce dedupe, plan hash must match, and only CODING/TESTING/FIXING/PUSHING or the `pushed`/`failed` events. The `pushed` event also carries `changed_files` and `suite_modified`. A callback can never claim READY_FOR_REVIEW or DEPLOYED.

**NOTHING DISAPPEARS.** Every transition is `UPDATE ... WHERE status = <from>` plus an `su_audit` row. The watchdog (`jobs.startWatchdog`, production only, 60 s, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claims for multiple instances) fails preparation interrupted by a restart (20 min), a dispatch that never started (15 min, checked against the run list by `run-name: SpeakUp job <id>`), a run that ended without reporting (75 min, with its conclusion), moves READY_FOR_REVIEW to DEPLOYING when the PR is merged on GitHub (CANCELLED if closed unmerged), and DEPLOYING to DEPLOYED when `RENDER_GIT_COMMIT` equals or contains the merge commit. "Why did the AI make this change?" = `GET /factory/jobs/:id/trace`: command transcript, source meetings, approved requirements with quotes, approver, plan hash, full audit chain.

**Project Registry** (`su_projects`, seeded per operator tenant, fill-only): ringlypro (`src`), speakup, orbup, jobmd, jobup, virtual-chamber — all `digit2ai/RinglyPro-CRM`, `main`. A project created from the API starts with `read,prepare` only. `test_commands` accept only `node|npx jest|npm test` with no shell metacharacters.

**Security changes to existing SpeakUp:** session cookie `SameSite=None` -> `Lax`; tokens signed and verified with `audience: 'speakup'`; login rate limit (10 / 15 min per IP+email, and 25 / 15 min per email alone, since the IP header can be spoofed against the Render origin); `PUBLIC_ASSET` no longer exempts `/api/*.js` paths from the cookie gate; every factory mutation requires `X-SpeakUp: 1` + same-host Origin. `SPEAKUP_SEED_USERS=off` skips the owner password force-sync (SIT uses it, because the local `.env` points at a production database).

**Endpoints** (`/speakup/api/v1/factory`): `GET /overview` · `POST /command` · `GET /jobs`, `GET /jobs/:id`, `GET /jobs/:id/trace`, `POST /jobs/:id/execute|merge|cancel` · `GET /jobs/:id/events`, `GET /jobs/:id/diff` · `PATCH /recordings/:id/session` · `GET|POST /recordings/:id/intel`, `PATCH /recordings/:id/intel/items/:itemId` · `GET /search` · `GET /documents/:id` · `GET|POST /projects`, `PATCH /projects/:key` · machine: `GET /brief/:jobId`, `POST /callback`, `POST /progress-log`. Deep links: `/speakup/?job=<id>`, `/speakup/?mode=command|meeting|note|architect`.

**EVERY JOB THAT CAN FAIL MUST BE ABLE TO SAY WHY.** The verify job had no failure reporter, so when a patch would not apply the reason was written on that VM and died there: the phone showed the shared report-failure message about the PUSH job, which had not run. It now reports with the NARROW progress token (never the factory secret — it applies untrusted patch data and runs the tests), exactly as the build job does. The offline suite asserts all four jobs report their own failure, and that the two untrusted ones do it with the progress token.

**Tests:** `node verticals/speakup/test-offline.js` -> **655/655** (no DB, no keys; runs in CI; includes the workflow job-isolation greps and real apply-patch/guard runs against a scratch git repo) · `node verticals/speakup/test-console-flow.js` -> **103/103** (browser; what the factory screen shows on open) · `node verticals/speakup/sit-factory.js` -> **198/198** (DB, fake GitHub, throwaway tenants, cleans up; covers the audience check, single-use brief token, progress-token limits, the merge gate, and the plan-then-approved path end to end: an instruction that dispatches nothing, a revision minting a new hash, a replaced plan refused, a replayed approval refused) · `node verticals/speakup/sit.js` -> **22/22 when it can sign in** (the app that remains; the removed editing endpoints are asserted ABSENT, not merely unused). **From a laptop it currently stops at login** — 2 passed, 3 failed, identical on the commit before the loop fix, so it is environmental, not a regression: it runs with `SPEAKUP_SEED_USERS=off` against the production database, so it cannot force-sync the owner password, and the local `.env` password no longer matches the one set on Render. NOT covered locally: the model paths, the real GitHub API, a real Claude Code run.

**Owner setup to open the execution door** (until then SpeakUp captures, analyzes and prepares, and shows the list of what is missing):
1. Render: `SPEAKUP_JWT_SECRET` (random, its own value), `SPEAKUP_TEAM_PASSWORD` (private, 12+), `SPEAKUP_EXEC_PHRASE` (4+ words), `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_SECRET` (random 32+ chars), `SPEAKUP_GITHUB_TOKEN` (fine-grained PAT on digit2ai/RinglyPro-CRM: Actions RW, Pull requests RW, Contents R — Contents RW only if merging from the phone).
2. GitHub repository secrets: `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_SECRET` (same value as Render) and ONE Claude credential: `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (subscription, `claude setup-token`) or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (API balance).
3. Recommended: a ruleset on `main` restricting updates with bypass for repository admins, so a workflow token can never update main even if the workflow were changed.

**Environment Variables:**
- `SPEAKUP_JWT_SECRET` — now REQUIRED for execution and must differ from `JWT_SECRET` (sessions still work without it, via the audience check).
- `SPEAKUP_EXEC_PHRASE` / `SPEAKUP_EXEC_PHRASE_SHA256` — the private execution phrase (plaintext must be 4+ words and 20+ characters). No default; unset = execution CLOSED.
- `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_SECRET` — HMAC key shared with the GitHub workflow (brief + callbacks). No default; unset = CLOSED.
- `SPEAKUP_GITHUB_TOKEN` — server-side GitHub token. Unset = CLOSED. `SPEAKUP_GITHUB_API` overrides the API base (tests).
- `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_ALLOWED_EMAILS` (default `mstagg@digit2ai.com`) — who may run the factory (must also be role admin).
- `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_MODEL` (`claude-opus-5`) — model Claude Code uses in Actions. `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_MAX_TURNS` (80).
- `SPEAKUP_INTEL_MODEL` (`claude-sonnet-5`) · `SPEAKUP_PLAN_MODEL` (`claude-opus-5`) · intent fallback uses `SPEAKUP_MODEL`. Reuse `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; unset = labelled heuristic intel and plan.
- `SPEAKUP_PUBLIC_URL` (`https://aiagent.ringlypro.com/speakup`) — trace link in PRs. `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_REPO` (`digit2ai/RinglyPro-CRM`) — repo for seeded projects. `SPEAKUP_DEFAULT_PROJECT` (`ringlypro`). `SPEAKUP_TZ` (`America/New_York`) — "yesterday", "this morning".
- `SPEAKUP_AUTO_MERGE` (`on`) — a console job with green tests merges its own PR, so Render deploys it. `off` leaves every PR for review.
- `SPEAKUP_AUTO_RUN` (`on`) — the console dispatches a prepared plan itself; `off` restores the approve-with-phrase tap.
- `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_MAX_EVENTS` (4000) — activity lines kept per job.
- `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_POLLER` (`on` forces the watchdog outside production, `off` stops it) · `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_PREPARE_TIMEOUT_MIN` (20) · `_DISPATCH_TIMEOUT_MIN` (15) · `_PROGRESS_TIMEOUT_MIN` (75) · `_DEPLOY_TIMEOUT_MIN` (60).
- `SPEAKUP_SEED_USERS` (`off` skips seeding the owner account; SIT only). `SPEAKUP_REPO_ROOT` — override the scanned checkout.
- GitHub Actions: secrets `SPEAKUP_FACTORY_SECRET` and either `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (subscription) or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (API credit); variable `SPEAKUP_BASE_URL` (default `https://aiagent.ringlypro.com/speakup`).

## AI Radar — capture AI discoveries from the phone share sheet (folder: airadar)

**Purpose:** The owner's personal log of AI products spotted while scrolling. See something AI-shaped on Instagram / Facebook / TikTok / X, hit Share, and it lands in an inbox with **company name, company website and a short description already drafted**; you correct whatever the draft got wrong and move on. Search, filter, rate and export the whole log later. Login-only, no public signup, no billing. Mounted at `/airadar`. English, emoji-free.

**Location:** `verticals/airadar/` — self-contained Express Router, own Sequelize via `src/db.js` (`CRM_DATABASE_URL || DATABASE_URL`). Tables auto-create on boot via `sync({alter:false})`; canonical migration `verticals/airadar/migrations/20260726_airadar_tables.sql`. Multi-tenant (`tenant_id` = user id), `ar_` prefix: `ar_users, ar_items, ar_enrichments`. New columns ensured via idempotent `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` in `index.js` init.

**Live:** app `/airadar/` · login `/airadar/login` · share target `/airadar/share` · health `/airadar/health` · debug `/debug/airadar-error`. Installable PWA (manifest + sw.js; network-first, never caches `/api/`, never intercepts `/share`).

**IT IS A BUCKET, NOT A FORM.** The product this replaces is the owner's WhatsApp "Message yourself" chat: tap share, it is saved, back to scrolling. **Nothing blocks the save** — no form, no confirmation, no page fetch and no model call inside the request (`services/save.js` `saveLink()` inserts the row and returns; SIT asserts capture answers in well under 2.5s, typically ~70ms). Labelling happens **after** the response via `enrichLater()` fire-and-forget, which fills only fields that are still empty and flips `ar_items.enrich_status` `pending → done|failed`. The list shows "reading the link" on a pending row and quietly refreshes every 3s until nothing is pending. If enrichment fails the link is still saved — that is the whole contract. Do not reintroduce a capture-time form.

**Two ways in — because iOS has no Web Share Target:**
- **Android / desktop Chrome:** the manifest declares `share_target` → `GET /airadar/share`, which **saves server-side and redirects to `/airadar/?saved=<id>`** (toast + highlighted row). Only a share carrying no link at all falls through to the add sheet.
- **iPhone:** a one-time Shortcut POSTs to `POST /airadar/api/v1/capture?key=<capture_token>` — the only route outside the cookie gate, authenticated by the per-user `ar_users.capture_token` (rotatable from the app, unique-indexed). Saves silently without opening a browser. Same endpoint powers the desktop bookmarklet. The in-app **Setup** sheet renders the exact address, the Shortcut steps and a copy-to-clipboard bookmarklet.
- The list itself reads like a chat of saved links (thumbnail, headline, host, relative time, Open button). The headline degrades honestly: company name, else the post title, else the bare link — never a placeholder.

**AI auto-enrich** (`src/services/enrich.js` + `src/services/metadata.js`): fetches the shared page (dependency-free og:/twitter:/`<title>` parser, 9s timeout, private-IP ranges refused), then Claude Haiku drafts `{company_name, company_url, description, category, tags}`. **Honesty is enforced in code, not just the prompt:**
- `company_url` is validated against the candidate list — the model cannot return a URL that was not in the evidence, and a social host is never proposed as a company site.
- Login-walled reels return their own brand as the page title ("Instagram", "TikTok - Make Your Day"); that noise is scrubbed, so the draft comes back **blank with `needs_review:true`** and states why, rather than filing a company called "Instagram".
- No `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` = the labelled heuristic path (`enriched_by:'heuristic'`, `is_simulated:true`), shown in the UI as a "no model" chip.
- Typing over any of the three core fields sets `enriched_by:'manual'`, clears `is_simulated` and clears the review flag — a human vouched for it. Re-enrich only ever fills fields that are still empty.
- Every draft is written to `ar_enrichments` with the page metadata actually fetched, so a suggestion can be re-read.

**REST API (`/airadar/api/v1/*`):** auth `POST /auth/login|logout`, `GET /auth/me` (returns the capture token), `POST /auth/rotate-capture-token` · items `GET /items` (q + status/category/platform/tag/needs_review filters), `POST /items` (opt `auto_enrich`), `GET /items/stats`, `GET /items/export?format=csv|json|md`, `GET|PATCH|DELETE /items/:id`, `POST /items/:id/enrich` (opt `apply`) · `POST /enrich` (draft, saves nothing) · `POST|GET /capture?key=` (token-authed).

**SIT:** `node verticals/airadar/sit.js` → **60/60** (auth gate, instant-save share target, capture latency ceiling, background labelling settling out of `pending`, second-hop provenance, platform detection, honesty flags, private-network refusal, search/filter/stats, capture + rotation invalidation, all three export formats, cross-tenant 404s). Zero external keys — runs green on the heuristic path.

**Environment Variables:**
- `AIRADAR_JWT_SECRET` — signs the `airadar_token` cookie (falls back to `JWT_SECRET`), 30d. SET on prod.
- `AIRADAR_MODEL` — Anthropic model for enrichment. Default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`. Reuses `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; unset key = labelled heuristic drafts (the app still works end to end).
- `AIRADAR_PASSWORD` — owner password force-synced on boot (falls back to `SPEAKUP_TEAM_PASSWORD`, default `Palindrome@7`).

## AI Readiness Department — a department inside the MCP Brain (folder: ai-readiness)

**Purpose:** take a CEO from fear to confidence about adopting AI, and leave them holding a personalised AI Readiness Roadmap plus a next step small enough to say yes to in the room. Five agents behind one Brain, presented end to end by a human sponsor. Mounted at `/ai-readiness`. Bilingual EN/ES, emoji-free.

**Location:** `verticals/ai-readiness/` — self-contained Express Router, own Sequelize via `src/db.js` (`CRM_DATABASE_URL || DATABASE_URL`). Tables auto-create on boot via `sync({alter:false})`; canonical migration `verticals/ai-readiness/migrations/20260808_ai_readiness_tables.sql`. Multi-tenant (`tenant_id` = sponsor id), `air_` prefix: `air_sponsors, air_engagements, air_answers, air_findings, air_roadmaps, air_calls, air_approvals`.

**Live:** sponsor console `/ai-readiness/` · login `/ai-readiness/login` · the CEO's read-only roadmap `/ai-readiness/roadmap/:token` · health `/ai-readiness/health` · debug `/debug/ai-readiness-error`.

### The crew — five agents, 22 tools, one gateway
| Agent | Overcomes | Namespace |
|---|---|---|
| **Readiness Director** | Not knowing where to start | `readiness_director.*` |
| **Cost Comfort Agent** | The fear of cost | `cost_comfort.*` |
| **Risk Comfort Agent** | The fear of risk | `risk_comfort.*` |
| **Data Readiness Agent** | The fear that the data is not good enough | `data_readiness.*` |
| **Roadmap Builder** | Not knowing what the actual next step is | `roadmap_builder.*` |

`src/brain.js` is the department gateway — same doctrine as the standalone MCP Brain (`github.com/digit2ai/mcp-brain`): five gates (agent enablement, channel allow-list, min trust, role allow-list, daily cost cap), `tenant_id` injected from session context and DELETED from tool arguments, human approval queue, full audit to `air_calls` including denials. `listTools` computes effective trust exactly as `callTool` does — do not let those drift.

**THE AGENT ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING AND MUST NOT BE PARALLELISED.** Data runs first (its blocking-gap count becomes remediation hours in the cost model), then Cost (its Phase 1 scope is what Risk writes guardrails around), then Risk, then the Roadmap Builder. Fanning them out concurrently leaves each guessing at the others' output and the three lanes quietly disagree about which processes are in the pilot.

### Honesty enforced in code, not in prompts
- **The model writes prose; it never writes a number.** `src/services/llm.js` hands the engines' computed figures to Claude as facts, then verifies the output: text introducing a figure the engines did not produce, or any guarantee language, is **discarded** in favour of the deterministic prose. No `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` = deterministic prose labeled `narrative_by:'heuristic'`; the figures are byte-identical either way (SIT asserts this).
- **Every dollar traces to an interview answer.** No industry benchmarks in the savings math. An unstated leak is omitted, never estimated. Assumptions live in `engines/cost.js` each carrying a `basis` string rendered into the deliverable.
- **`run_department` REFUSES to analyse while a required interview answer is missing**, and names which. This is the whole difference from the artifact the CEO was oversold last time.
- **Phase 1 excludes regulated data, customer-facing output and zero-error-tolerance work by rule.** Phase 3 is **never priced** — costing a transformation against unknowns is the fabricated number that teaches CEOs to distrust these documents.
- **The verdict is not an average**: a blocking data item dominates two green lanes. And there is **always a safe next step** — a red scorecard yields a smaller step, never "come back later".
- **Mitigation vs guardrail**: every risk carries both, plus an owner and evidence. `publish_to_ceo` is approval-gated — the department obeys the "AI does not act without a human" rule it is selling.

### The deliverable
A Red/Yellow/Green scorecard (Cost Comfort · Risk Comfort · Data Readiness), a three-phase roadmap where each phase carries cost, risk level, data requirements, timeline, success metrics and a **gate**, a safe next step sized under the CEO's stated exposure ceiling, and a **sponsor talk track** (what to say, what to watch for, prepared answers to the five objections that actually get raised). The talk track is deliberately **absent from the CEO's copy** — it includes how to read the room.

**SIT:** `node verticals/ai-readiness/sit.js` → **95/95**, zero external keys. Asserts the invariants above, not the happy path: determinism with and without a model, the refusal on missing inputs, regulated work never entering Phase 1, the approval gate not running its handler, a model-supplied `tenant_id` reading as "not found", and denied calls still writing an audit row.

**Doctrine:** `mcp-brain/agents/ai-readiness-department.md` (portable, vertical-neutral).

**Environment Variables:**
- `AIR_JWT_SECRET` — signs the `air_token` sponsor cookie (falls back to `JWT_SECRET`), 30d. SET on prod.
- `AIR_MODEL` — Anthropic model for the executive-summary prose. Default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`. Reuses `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; unset = deterministic prose, identical figures.
